It reads `learning_runtime_commits`, not `learning_runtime_sessions.state`: a session is one row per package+assignment and `start_learning_runtime_session` reactivates it on every launch, so a state-based gate would forget a finished package the moment the learner reopened it. `canAdvanceCourseStep` gains the same gate (a `scorm` step had no gate at all, because the package's own completion used to end the course), the player says which completion it is recording, and it now reflects a package finished on an earlier visit instead of asking for it again. **(3) A dead offline video link.** `prepare_offline_course_bundle` returns the block's stored locator, which for an org-authored video is `storage://course-videos/<path>` -- not a URL -- and `OfflineCourse` rendered it as an href. Dead even online, and doubly so since the bucket went private in `20260714233041`. It resolves through `useCourseVideoUrl` now, the same as the live player. **(4) A 15-minute URL with no way back.** The signed course-video URL lasts 15 minutes and plenty of lessons are longer, so a learner watching straight through hit an expired signature mid-playback: the browser reported a media error nothing was listening for, the video stopped with no message, and a reload lost the place. `useCourseVideoUrl` exposes a `refresh()`; the player re-signs on the media error (capped at two attempts, so a deleted object says so instead of spinning), saves `currentTime` first and restores it on the new URL. Deliberately NOT refetch-on-focus: that would swap the `<video>` src every time a learner tabs away and back. 9 pgTAP assertions in `package_step_defers_completion.test.sql` and 3 unit assertions on the step gate. `phase4_governed_learning.test.sql`'s two bridge assertions were inverted in the same change set -- they asserted the defect ("completion bridge flips the assignment to completed") and now assert that a package result completes nothing. |

It died inside `parse()`, before staging a row, which is exactly what the ledger shows (heartbeat +10 s, 0 rows, no error -- an OOM kill runs no catch block). Now streamed through `CsvParseStream` and staged in 1000-row chunks: same 80,355 entries at **88 MB above baseline** and **1.00 s of CPU** against the platform's 2 s. Durability: a `stage_cursor` + `last_progress_at` on the run, written every 10 chunks, so a pass that runs out of budget parks and the hourly continuation finishes it; the cursor carries a fingerprint of the source file and is ignored unless it matches; the worker reuses the open run's correlation instead of minting a fresh one, so a retry continues the run rather than opening a second beside it; and `app_private.reconcile_stalled_exclusion_refresh_runs()` (called by the watchdog) closes runs with no progress for six hours, which closed the 2026-08-12 row. 10 Deno tests on the resume rules in `_shared/leieStaging.test.ts` (`screen-exclusions` previously had none) and 17 pgTAP assertions in `exclusion_refresh_resume.test.sql`. ## Sequence

Single-threaded, because there is one person. This is an order, not a schedule — dates
would be fiction. Do not start the next block until the previous one is actually done.

**1. Live truth.** ~~SG-1~~ closed — A1, A2, A5 remain (ops-only).
A real signup now gets full functionality the moment it signs up — invite staff, complete
a course, export a binder, and *receive real email/SMS* — with no separate pilot-enrollment
step in the way. What is left in this block is ordinary launch operations (live Stripe
pricing, a signed BAA), not a product gate.

**2. Wire up what is already built.** ~~B1~~, B3, ~~B5~~, ~~D3~~, ~~D5~~.
Each is a half-built row: the code exists, no surface calls it. B1, B5, D3, and D5 are now
wired and done. B3 remains (real vendor packages — owner drop-path). This is the cheapest
block on the list and the one most likely to be skipped, because none of it looks like
progress. Doing it before new features is how the pile stops growing. (C2 was the fifth
and is now closed.)

**3. Finish SG-2 install → activate.**
Counsel cleared option 2 and the PA personnel templates are seeded, with the install UI wired
in Enterprise Foundation. Next step is not more debate; it is to install a PA draft, complete
guarded review and fixture verification, and activate a governed version -- activation no
longer waits on a live-shadow period (2026-08-04). Until that evidence exists, the copilot
remains a drafting aid for Pennsylvania.

**4. Product depth.** ~~B4~~ done, ~~E1~~ done, ~~C5~~ done.
C5 is complete: first-class `citation_ref` on packet items, RPC/UI wired, regulation order prefers
structured citation then label parse. Only once 1–3 are settled for live use.

**Deliberately not in this list:** A5 (BAAs), because it depends on someone outside the
repo. Start it early precisely because it is the only thing here that can wait on another
person's calendar.

---

## Verification contract for any row marked `done`

1. Code on `main` (or a merged PR linked in the row notes)
2. Relevant unit / edge / e2e tests pass in CI
3. If user-visible: real or demo org exercise recorded
4. This file updated in the same change set — enforced by `check:planning-registers`

A row is `in_progress`, not `done`, when the mechanism exists but nothing calls it. This is
the most common way this register goes wrong: #355 produced four such rows at the time
(B1, B3, C2, D3), and the very next commit added a fifth by shipping
`QuarantinePackageDialog.tsx` with no importer (B5). Of those, B1, C2, and D3 have since
been finished. Recording built-but-unreachable code as `done` is how a backlog stops
describing the product.

Ops-only rows close when runbook evidence exists outside the repo (do not commit customer
data). "Ops-only" means a different hat, not a different person — see "Operating reality"
above.

**On self-review.** Every gate in this list is one person checking their own work, so the
checks that do not depend on a second reader are the ones carrying real weight: CI, the
pgTAP suite, `check:all`, and this register's own freshness check. Treat a mechanical gate
that a second account merely unlocked (`approve_regulatory_rule_version`) as unverified,
and say so in the row rather than counting it as review.
